Dear Sir/ Ma’am,  
 
Subject: Submission of Annual Report for FY 2025 -26 and Notice convening the 19th Annual General 
Meeting . 
 
We wish to inform you that the 19th Annual General Meeting ("AGM")  of the Members of Emmvee 
Photovoltaic Power Limited ("Company")  is scheduled to be held on Friday, September 25, 2026 at 4:00 
PM (IST) through Video Conferencing ("VC")  / Other Audio Visual Means ("OAVM") , in compliance 
with the applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing Regulations”)  and the relevant circulars issued by the 
Ministry of Corporate Affairs and the Securities and Exchange Board of India.  
 
Pursuant to Regulation 34 of the SEBI Listing Regulations, please find enclosed herewith the Annual 
Report of the Company for the financial year 2025 -26, along with the Notice convening the 19th AGM.  
 
The Notice of AGM and the Annual Report are being dispatched electronically to those Members whose 
e-mail addresses are registered with the Company, Depositories, Registrar and Share Transfer Agent 
and/or Depository Participants. For Members whose e -mail a ddresses are not registered, a separate 
communication is being sent providing the web -link and exact path for accessing the Notice of AGM 
and the Annual Report for the financial year 2025 -26. 
 
The Annual Report for the financial year 2025 -26 and the Notice convening the 19th AGM are also available 
on the website of the Company at https://www.emmveepv.com/investors . 
 
Kindly take the above information on record.  
 
Thanking You,  
 
For and on behalf of Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited  
(Formerly known as Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Private Limited)

As the solar sector enters its next phase of growth, 
reliability alone is no longer enough. Manufacturers 
must also possess the ability to scale efficiently, 
respond quickly to evolving market requirements and 
maintain greater control across the value chain. This is 
where integration becomes a defining advantage. By 
enhancing manufacturing synergies, improving quality 
oversight and strengthening supply-chain visibility, 
integration creates a platform for sustainable and 
scalable growth.THE SOLAR INDUSTRY IS ENTERING A 
NEW PHASE; WHAT BEGAN AS A DRIVE TO 
EXPAND RENEWABLE ENERGY CAPACITY 
IS NOW CENTRED ON BUILDING A 
RESILIENT AND SELF-RELIANT ECOSYSTEM 
CAPABLE OF SUPPORTING LONG-TERM 
ENERGY NEEDS. AS INDIA ACCELERATES 
ITS RENEWABLE ENERGY AMBITIONS, 
THE FOCUS IS SHIFTING TOWARDS 
CREATING MANUFACTURING CAPABILITIES 
THAT COMBINE SCALE, QUALITY AND 
SUPPLY-CHAIN RESILIENCE. THIS SHIFT IS 
REDEFINING THE INDUSTRY'S PRIORITIES 
AND CREATING NEW OPPORTUNITIES FOR 
INTEGRATED MANUFACTURERS TO PLAY A 
LARGER ROLE IN THE COUNTRY'S ENERGY 
TRANSFORMATION.
Built on Reliability. 
Scaling through Integration.Recognising this opportunity, we are 
advancing our integrated manufacturing 
strategy through expanded cell 
manufacturing capabilities, backward 
integration initiatives and the proposed 
6 GW integrated solar cell and module 
manufacturing facility at Devanahalli.The next phase of our journey will be defined 
by the same principles that have shaped 
our progress thus far. Reliability remains the 
foundation upon which we have earned the 
confidence of customers and stakeholders, 
while deeper integration is enabling us to 
scale with greater agility, efficiency and 
purpose. Together, these strengths position 
us to capture emerging opportunities and 
play a larger role in shaping the future of 
solar energy.
Against this backdrop, reliability has 
emerged as a critical differentiator. 
In an industry where continued 
performance matters as much as 
efficiency, confidence is built through 
consistency, quality and dependable 
execution. At Emmvee, these principles 
have guided our journey from the 
very beginning, helping us establish a 
strong reputation for delivering solar 
solutions that perform reliably across 
diverse operating environments.

WHO WE ARE
At the Core of India's 
Clean Energy Transition
AS ONE OF INDIA’S LEADING SOLAR PHOTOVOLTAIC 
MANUFACTURERS, WE COMBINE INTEGRATED EXPERTISE 
ACROSS SOLAR CELLS AND MODULES WITH A PROVEN 
TRACK RECORD OF QUALITY AND INNOVATION. 
Driven by the growing momentum of the energy transition, we are enhancing our manufacturing capabilities and expanding 
into new markets to power the next phase of sustainable growth. Our products are designed to deliver reliable performance 
and maximise energy generation, creating lasting value for customers worldwide.
Supporting this growth is our sustained investments in technology, infrastructure and operational excellence. This has deepened 
our capabilities across the solar value chain, enabling us to deliver high-performance solar solutions at scale. This foundation 
has reinforced our competitiveness, broadened our market reach and accelerated our growth trajectory.

A Journey Shaped by 
Purpose and PerformanceCHAIRMAN’S MESSAGE
Dear Shareholders,
IT GIVES ME GREAT PLEASURE TO 
PRESENT EMMVEE PHOTOVOLTAIC 
POWER LIMITED’S FIRST ANNUAL 
REPORT AS A PUBLICLY LISTED 
COMPANY. 
Our listing on the National Stock Exchange and the Bombay Stock 
Exchange on November 18, 2025, marked a defining milestone 
in our journey, reflecting decades of steadfast commitment, 
disciplined execution and an unwavering belief in India’s solar 
future. It represents the culmination of years of dedication in an 
industry that has demanded resilience, patience and conviction. 
I thank each of our shareholders for their trust in Emmvee, and I 
remain deeply committed to honouring that confidence through 
our actions and performance, year after year.Understanding the Forces Shaping Tomorrow
Escalating trade tensions, supply chain bottlenecks and geopolitical 
uncertainties created a challenging global backdrop, weighing 
on market sentiment. Yet, India stood strong, recording a GDP 
growth rate of 7.7%, driven by robust manufacturing investment, 
infrastructure expansion, rising energy demand and a policy 
framework that is directing capital towards productive sectors. 
The solar industry is evolving alongside these broader shifts. As 
markets mature, competitiveness is being defined by far more than 
manufacturing scale alone. The ability to integrate operations, 
deploy advanced technologies, deliver consistently and build 
trusted customer relationships is becoming increasingly important. 
Companies that combine these capabilities with disciplined execution 
will be best placed to lead the industry’s next phase of growth.
Accelerating India’s Journey to a Cleaner Future 
India’s energy transition has evolved into a national industrial 
priority, supported by a policy framework that is fostering both 
domestic manufacturing and sustained demand. Initiatives such 
as the Approved List of Models and Manufacturers, the Production Linked Incentive Scheme, Domestic Content Requirements and 
the phased extension of ALMM across the solar value chain are 
creating a more resilient manufacturing ecosystem. At the same 
time, programmes such as PM Surya Ghar and PM-KUSUM are 
accelerating solar adoption across households and agriculture, 
complementing the rapid expansion of utility-scale installations.
India’s aspiration to achieve 500 GW of non-fossil fuel capacity by 
2030 places solar at the centre of its energy future. We recognised 
this direction early and have steadily built our capabilities to 
align with it. During the year under review, we expanded our 
module manufacturing capacity, enhanced our cell manufacturing 
platform, commissioned new capacity at Sulibele and advanced the 
development of our integrated cell and module facility at Devanahalli. 
These investments position us to participate in the next phase of 
India’s solar growth with greater scale, stronger integration and 
enhanced execution capabilities.
The Making of Emmvee
Where It All Began 
When I entered the solar industry in 1992, renewable energy was 
still regarded as a distant possibility rather than a commercial reality. 
The ecosystem was nascent, infrastructure was limited, markets 
were uncertain and policy support had yet to emerge. Even so, I 
was convinced that solar energy would one day reshape the way 
the world generates and consumes power.
That belief laid the foundation for Emmvee Solar Systems in 1996 
and, later, Emmvee toughened glass and Photovoltaics Private 
Limited in 2007. What began with a modest manufacturing base has 
evolved into one of India’s leading integrated solar manufacturers, 
with 10.3 GW of installed module capacity and 2.94 GW of solar 
cell capacity. Every milestone has been shaped by disciplined 
execution, prudent investment and an unwavering focus on building 
enduring capabilities. 
A New Chapter
With our listing, we entered a new phase of our journey, carrying 
forward the foundation we had built over the years and enhancing 
our ability to invest with confidence in the opportunities ahead. The 
INR 2,90,000 Lakhs raised through the IPO enabled us to prepay a 
significant portion of our debt, leaving us with a net debt-to-equity 
ratio of negative 0.06x as at 31 March 2026.
Shaping the Future
As India accelerates its clean energy ambitions, we are focused on 
expanding our manufacturing capabilities to support this national 
transformation. We are developing a 6 GW integrated cell and 
module manufacturing facility at Devanahalli, Bengaluru, which 
will take our total capacity to 16.3 GW of modules and 8.94 GW 
of cells by FY2028. Land has already been acquired and funding 
from IREDA has been sanctioned. In parallel, we are evaluating 
phased expansion into ingot and wafer manufacturing to deepen 
vertical integration and reduce our dependence on imported 
upstream components. People Who Make the Difference
Behind every milestone we have achieved is the collective effort 
of our people, whose dedication, expertise and commitment have 
shaped Emmvee’s journey. Today, our team of more than 3,800 
people, engineers, technicians, supply chain professionals, quality 
specialists and corporate staff work together with a shared sense 
of purpose. We remain committed to providing an environment 
where our people can learn, grow and thrive. Through sustained 
investments in capability development, workplace safety and 
employee well-being, we are building an organisation that is 
equipped not only for the opportunities of today but also for the 
demands of tomorrow.
A Commitment Beyond Business
A deep sense of responsibility guides the way we grow. We 
believe that business success must be accompanied by thoughtful 
stewardship of natural resources and meaningful engagement with 
the communities around us. Today, 88% of the energy consumed 
across our manufacturing operations is sourced from renewables. 
We also maintain zero liquid discharge across our facilities, achieve 
92% water recovery and manufacture our modules using lead-free 
aluminium frames. 
Beyond our operations, the Emmvee Foundation works with 
communities across Chikkaballapur and Bengaluru to improve 
access to clean water, healthcare and road safety. These efforts 
reflect our belief that the value we create should extend beyond our 
business and contribute positively to the society in which we operate.
The Way Forward
As we begin the next phase of our journey, we do so with a clear 
understanding of the responsibility that comes with being a publicly 
listed company. Our shareholders rightly expect transparency, disciplined 
capital allocation, sound governance and consistent execution, and 
these principles will continue to guide every decision we make.
The opportunities before us are significant, but so are the 
expectations. Technology will evolve, competition will intensify and 
global markets will remain dynamic. In this environment, success will 
depend on disciplined execution, operational excellence, financial 
prudence and the trust we earn from our customers, partners and 
shareholders.
At Emmvee, we remain committed to deepening our integrated 
manufacturing capabilities, advancing technology, enhancing 
operational efficiency and maintaining a resilient balance sheet as 
we build for the future. 
To our shareholders, customers, partners and employees, thank 
you for your confidence, commitment and support. The foundation 
we have built together gives us the confidence to embrace the 
opportunities ahead and contribute meaningfully to a cleaner, 
more energy-secure future.
Warm Regards,
Manjunatha Donthi Venkatarathnaiah 
Chairman and Managing Director

Refining Our  
Blueprint for ScaleCEO’S MESSAGE
Dear Shareholders,
FY 2025-26 WAS A DEFINING YEAR 
FOR EMMVEE. OUR SUCCESSFUL 
LISTING IN NOVEMBER 2025 MARKED 
AN IMPORTANT MILESTONE IN 
OUR EVOLUTION, OPENING A NEW 
CHAPTER AS A PUBLICLY LISTED 
ENTERPRISE WHILE REINFORCING 
OUR COMMITMENT TO DISCIPLINED 
EXECUTION, TRANSPARENCY AND 
SUSTAINED VALUE CREATION. 
It was also a year in which we translated strategic intent into 
measurable outcomes by expanding manufacturing capacity, 
enhancing operational efficiency, reinforcing our financial position 
and securing a stronger pipeline for future growth.These achievements reflect more than the progress of a single year. 
They demonstrate the strength of the foundation we have built, the 
resilience of our business model and our ability to execute with 
consistency in a rapidly evolving solar landscape. As we build on 
this momentum, we remain focused on expanding our capabilities, 
deepening integration across the value chain and creating enduring 
value for our stakeholders.
Defining Our Competitive Edge
The progress we achieved during the year is underpinned by 
the distinctive strengths of our business model. As India’s solar 
manufacturing sector continues to evolve, competitive advantage 
increasingly depends not only on scale, but also on the ability to 
deliver consistent quality, technological advancement and supply-
chain resilience.
In this environment, Emmvee stands among India’s few integrated 
solar photovoltaic manufacturers, combining solar cell and module 
production within a consolidated manufacturing cluster in Bengaluru. 
This integrated platform enhances operational coordination, provides 
greater control over product quality, improves cost efficiencies 
and enables faster responses to changing customer requirements.Our manufacturing capabilities are complemented by a technology-
led approach. We operate with 100% TOPCon technology across our 
module capacity, supported by our collaboration with Fraunhofer ISE, 
one of Europe’s foremost solar research institutions. This commitment 
to excellence is further validated through independent assessments, 
with Emmvee among a select group of global manufacturers to 
have successfully completed seven tests under Kiwa PVEL’s Product 
Qualification Programme for a single product type. Complementing 
these achievements, our warranty claim rates have remained below 
0.016% of revenue, reflecting the consistency and reliability of our 
products. Together, these credentials reinforce customer confidence, 
enhance our competitive differentiation and support the continued 
expansion of our market presence.
When Strategy Delivers Results
The strength of our integrated manufacturing platform and disciplined 
execution was clearly reflected in our financial performance during 
the year. Revenue from operations increased by 116% year-on-year 
to INR 5,04,988 Lakhs, driven by higher production volumes, the 
commissioning of two new module manufacturing lines and the 
first full year of contributions from our solar cell operations. This 
growth, combined with the benefits of operating leverage, improved 
cell utilisation and greater integration across our manufacturing 
platform, contributed to a significant improvement in profitability. 
EBITDA grew 140% to INR 1,73,437 Lakhs, with margins improving 
from 31% to 34%. Profit after tax grew by 193% to INR 1,08,155 
Lakhs, resulting in a PAT margin of 21%. The strengthening of 
our financial profile was further supported by lower finance costs 
following the repayment of debt using proceeds from the IPO, 
providing additional flexibility to invest in our strategic priorities 
while maintaining a healthy balance sheet.
Building Capacity, Capability and Confidence
The operational changes in FY2026 laid the foundation for our 
next phase of growth. We commissioned two 2.5 GW solar module 
manufacturing lines at Sulibele, taking total module installed capacity 
to 10.3 GW, increasing two-fold from the previous year. This 
expansion translated into higher production, with module output 
increasing to 2,999 MW from 1,482 MW in the prior year. 
FY 2025-26 also marked our first full year of solar cell operations. 
Cell production reached 1,520 MW, nearly three times the previous 
year’s output, while capacity utilisation improved significantly over 
the course of the year. We also commenced production of G12R-
format TOPCon cells, enabling higher power-density module 
configurations. These advancements were complemented by a 
multi-year 4.5 GW TOPCon cell supply agreement, providing greater 
revenue visibility, while our overall order book expanded from 4.9 
GW at the beginning of the year to 9.4 GW as of 31 March 2026.
Advancing Technology. Enhancing Sustainability
Our collaboration with Fraunhofer ISE has progressed beyond 
the commercialisation of TOPCon cell manufacturing to focus 
on laser-enhanced contact optimisation and next-generation cell 
architectures. Internally, we strengthened quality assurance through AI-enabled multi-stage electroluminescence inspection across our 
production lines, improving defect detection at scale. 
At the same time, our transition to G12R-format modules is 
progressing smoothly, supported by manufacturing lines designed 
to accommodate the new format with minimal disruption. We also 
remain focused on improving manufacturing efficiency, including 
the optimisation of silver paste consumption, a significant cost 
component in solar cell production.
As we expand our operations, we remain equally focused on 
manufacturing responsibly. Resource efficiency has been embedded 
into the design and operation of our facilities, with 88% of our 
energy requirements sourced from renewable power. At Unit IV, 
our zero liquid discharge system enables 92% water recovery, 
while the use of lead-free aluminium frames across our modules 
reflects our continued efforts to reduce the environmental footprint 
of our products. 
Charting the Next Frontier
Having established a stronger manufacturing platform, our focus 
now shifts to the next stage of integration and capacity expansion. 
Construction of our 6 GW integrated cell and module manufacturing 
facility at Devanahalli is underway, with module line orders already 
finalised. We expect the module line to be commissioned by the 
end of calendar year 2026, followed by the cell line by the end 
of FY 2026-27. Upon completion, our total installed capacity will 
increase to 16.3 GW of modules and 8.94 GW of solar cells.
Beyond this expansion, we are evaluating the phased development 
of approximately 9 GW of ingot and wafer manufacturing capacity, 
with the first phase expected to commence in FY 2028-29. This will 
mark an important step towards achieving end-to-end backward 
integration, further enhancing our manufacturing capabilities and 
strengthening control across the solar value chain.
To Our Shareholders
The opportunities before us are both significant and enduring. Rising 
electricity demand, a supportive policy environment, increasing 
localisation and the adoption of higher-efficiency technologies 
are reshaping India’s solar manufacturing landscape. With an 
integrated business model, expanding capabilities and a clear 
strategic direction, we are well placed to contribute meaningfully 
to this transformation.
As we enter the next phase of our growth, we remain committed to 
disciplined execution, thoughtful capital allocation and delivering 
products that earn the confidence of our customers. I would like 
to thank our shareholders for their trust, our customers for their 
continued partnership and our employees for their dedication in 
making this a defining year for Emmvee. Together, we will continue 
to build a stronger, more integrated enterprise and create enduring 
value in the years ahead.
Regards,
Suhas Donthi Manjunatha 
Chief Executive Officer

Shaping the Future 
through a Financial LensCFO’S MESSAGE
Dear Shareholders,
FY 2025-26 WAS A YEAR OF 
EXCEPTIONAL FINANCIAL PROGRESS 
FOR EMMVEE, REFLECTING THE 
STRENGTH OF OUR OPERATING 
MODEL AND THE DISCIPLINED 
EXECUTION OF OUR STRATEGIC 
PRIORITIES.  
Alongside robust growth in revenue and profitability, we strengthened 
our balance sheet, enhanced financial flexibility and created a 
stronger platform to support the Company’s next phase of expansion.
As our first Annual Report following the Company’s listing, the year 
also marked an important step in reinforcing financial prowess, 
capital stewardship and prudent investment. The following sections 
provide an overview of our financial performance, the key factors 
that shaped our results and the priorities that will guide our capital 
allocation as we continue to scale our business.The Value We Delivered
The financial performance delivered in FY 2025-26 reflects the 
successful conversion of operational execution into measurable 
results. Revenue from operations grew by 116% to INR 5,04,988 
Lakhs, while EBITDA rose 140% to INR 1,73,437 Lakhs, resulting 
in an EBITDA margin at 34%. Profit after tax grew 193% to INR 
1,08,155 Lakhs, with PAT margin at 21% and Return on Capital 
Employed stood at 38%. Together, these results reflect the combined 
impact of capacity expansion, higher asset utilisation and robust 
cost management, supported by the strength of our integrated 
manufacturing model.
The Story Our Numbers Tell
A Stronger Financial Foundation
Alongside stronger operating performance, FY 2025-26 marked 
a significant strengthening of our balance sheet following the 
successful completion of the IPO. We raised INR 2,90,000 Lakhs, 
comprising INR 2,14,386 Lakhs of fresh issue proceeds and an offer for sale component at a price band of INR 206–217 per share. 
Approximately INR 1,62,129 Lakhs of the fresh issue proceeds was 
utilised to prepay term loans, significantly reducing leverage and 
lowering our finance costs. This deployment of capital resulted in 
a marked improvement across key financial indicators.  Our Net 
debt-to-equity moved from 2.55x at the end of FY2025 to negative 
0.06x by March 31, 2026, while the current ratio improved to 2.1x. 
at the end of FY 2024-25 to a net cash position of 0.06x as of 31 
March 2026, while the current ratio strengthened to 2.1x. Reflecting 
this enhanced financial profile, ICRA upgraded our credit rating 
twice during the year, from BBB+ to A- in August 2025, and further 
to A in January 2026, recognising our improved scale, stronger 
debt coverage metrics and lower leverage.
Our Markers of Progress
The quality of our financial performance is also reflected in several 
underlying indicators. Return on equity stood at 51%, reflecting both 
the profitability of the year and the evolving equity base post-IPO. 
Despite the expansion of our operations, finance costs declined as 
a result of lower borrowings, while the gross profit margin remained 
stable at approximately 45%, demonstrating effective management 
of material costs. Net working capital increased to INR 1,57,501 
Lakhs, in line with the significant growth in production volumes and 
revenue.Advancing Technology. Enhancing Sustainability.
Confidence Behind Our Outlook
Having established a stronger manufacturing platform, our focus 
now shifts to the next stage of integration and capacity expansion. 
Construction of our 6 GW integrated cell and module manufacturing 
facility at Devanahalli is underway, with module line orders already 
finalised. We expect the module line to be commissioned by the 
end of calendar year 2026, followed by the cell line by the end 
of FY 2026-27. Upon completion, our total installed capacity will 
increase to 16.3 GW of modules and 8.94 GW of solar cells.
Beyond this expansion, we are evaluating the phased development 
of approximately 9 GW of ingot and wafer manufacturing capacity, 
with the first phase expected to commence in FY 2028-29. This will 
mark an important step towards achieving end-to-end backward 
integration, further enhancing our manufacturing capabilities and 
strengthening control across the solar value chain.
To Our Shareholders
Our financial position is further supported by healthy revenue 
visibility. As of 31 March 2026, our order book stood at 9.4 GW, 
providing a strong foundation for future execution. During the 
year, we also secured a multi-year 4.5 GW TOPCon cell supply 
agreement extending to 2030, further strengthening long-term 
revenue visibility. In addition, the average order size among our ten 
largest customers increased from 121 MW in FY 2024-25 to 221 MW in FY 2025-26, reflecting deeper customer relationships and 
growing confidence in our manufacturing capabilities.
Investing in the Next Chapter
Our capital allocation priorities remain firmly aligned with our 
long-term growth plans. We have committed approximately INR 
4,50,000–4,80,000 Lakhs towards the development of our 6 
GW integrated solar cell and module manufacturing facility at 
Devanahalli, with investments phased in line with project milestones. 
Approximately 75–80% of the capital expenditure is expected to 
be deployed by March 2027, with the balance scheduled for FY 
2027-28 in accordance with retention payment timelines.
Beyond this project, we continue to evaluate the phased development 
of ingot and wafer manufacturing, with an estimated investment of 
INR 60,000–70,000 Lakhs per GW from FY 2028-29 onwards. 
As we pursue these opportunities, our priority will be to maintain a 
prudent capital structure, carefully balancing growth investments 
with earnings generation and prudent allocation. 
Delivering More Through Discipline
FY 2025-26 marked a significant step forward in strengthening 
Emmvee’s financial foundation. The progress achieved during the 
year has enhanced our ability to invest in future capacity, support 
the next phase of integration and pursue growth from a position 
of financial strength.
As we move ahead, our approach will remain unchanged. We 
will allocate capital with careful assessment, maintain a prudent 
balance sheet and uphold the financial rigour that underpins 
every investment decision. I thank our shareholders for their 
continued trust and confidence, and remain committed to delivering 
sustainable financial performance with transparency, accountability 
and responsible stewardship.
Regards,
Pawan Kumar Jain 
Chief Financial Officer

Meeting Energy 
needs at Every ScaleOUR PRODUCT PORTFOLIO
Photovoltaic Modules
Our photovoltaic module portfolio is designed to address the 
evolving requirements of utility-scale, commercial and industrial 
solar projects. Leveraging advanced technologies such as TOPCon   
and bifacial modules, we deliver solutions that maximise energy 
generation while ensuring operational reliability and long-term 
performance across varied climatic and operating conditions.Product Roadmap
Our product roadmap reflects the industry’s transition towards higher-efficiency, larger-format and lower-degradation modules. The 
Company’s TOPCon-led portfolio is designed to address the requirements of utility-scale, C&I and domestic-content-linked solar projects. 
Solar Cells
We manufacture advanced TOPCon solar cells that form the foundation of 
our photovoltaic module portfolio. The development of our cell manufacturing 
capabilities has enhanced integration across the solar value chain, providing 
greater control over product quality, technology adoption and supply 
chain reliability. 
With an installed TOPCon cell manufacturing capacity of 2.94 GW, we have 
further bolstered our manufacturing ecosystem and created a platform for 
innovation, capacity expansion and long-term growth.This capability is supported by advanced manufacturing processes, 
highly automated production lines and rigorous quality assurance 
standards that ensure consistent product excellence. With an 
installed module manufacturing capacity of 10.3 GW, we cater to 
solar developers, EPC contractors and businesses across India and 
international markets, enabling the wider adoption of clean energy 
through reliable, high-performance solar solutions.Multi Technology Portfolio Product Features

Scaling with India’s 
Solar Ambitions CAPITALISING ON THE OPPORTUNITY LANDSCAPE
OVER THE PAST DECADE, INDIA’S POWER SECTOR HAS UNDERGONE 
A SIGNIFICANT TRANSFORMATION, EVOLVING INTO A SYSTEM 
CHARACTERISED BY GREATER ADEQUACY, EXPANDED CAPACITY AND 
ENHANCED RELIABILITY. 
This progress reflects years of sustained investment, institutional reforms, regulatory discipline and policy continuity, 
creating a stronger foundation to support the country’s long-term development ambitions.
Against this backdrop, India’s growing economy, expanding industrial base and increasing electrification are 
driving a steady rise in electricity demand. During FY2025-26, the country successfully met a record peak power 
demand of 242.49 GW, while reducing the national energy shortage to just 0.03%.

of India's evolving energy landscape.

installed capacity
Renewable energy has progressed from an alternative power source 
into a key driver of India's capacity addition programme. With non-
fossil sources now accounting for more than half of the country's 
installed power capacity, the sector is playing an increasingly 
important role in meeting future energy needs.

=== page 14 ===
Policy-driven Demand Expansion
Government initiatives are improving solar adoption across 
residential, agricultural, commercial and utility-scale segments, 
creating multiple avenues of demand across the renewable energy 
landscape.
Key Drivers
	 PM Surya Ghar: Muft Bijli Yojana accelerating rooftop solar 
adoption through capital subsidies and targeting One Hundred 
Lakhs households
	 PM-KUSUM promoting the solarisation of agricultural pumps 
and feeder infrastructure
	 Renewable Purchase Obligations (RPOs) and annual bidding 
trajectories providing greater visibility for renewable energy 
project development
	 Green Open Access framework enabling commercial and 
industrial consumers to increase renewable energy procurement
	 Green Power Markets (GTAM and GDAM) supporting market-
based procurement of renewable energy.Infrastructure and Ecosystem Readiness
The expansion of renewable energy capacity is being complemented 
by significant investments in transmission and grid infrastructure. 
These developments are enhancing network preparedness and 
enabling the seamless integration of renewable power into the 
national electricity system.
Key Drivers
	 Green Energy Corridor Programme boosting renewable energy 
evacuation and grid connectivity
	 National Electricity Plan for Transmission (2023-32) targeting 
inter-regional transfer capacity of 168 GW and supporting the 
evacuation of up to 500 GW of renewable energy
	 Ongoing investments in transmission infrastructure improving 
grid reliability and facilitating large-scale solar deployment.Policy Support for Domestic Manufacturing
India’s policy framework is increasingly geared towards building 
a self-reliant solar manufacturing ecosystem through localisation, 
supply chain development and investments across the value chain.
Key Drivers
	 Production Linked Incentive (PLI) Scheme driving investments 
in high-efficiency solar PV manufacturing
	 Approved List of Models and Manufacturers (ALMM) 
supporting demand for domestically manufactured modules
	 Proposed ALMM framework for solar cells encouraging 
upstream localisation and integrated manufacturing
	 Domestic Content Requirement (DCR) programmes creating 
opportunities for local cell and module manufacturers
	 Basic Customs Duty and non-tariff measures reducing 
import dependence and improving competitiveness of 
domestic producers
	 Long-term renewable energy targets and net-zero commitments 
providing sustained visibility for manufacturing investments
India’s push towards 
localisation is 
strengthening the 
domestic solar 
manufacturing 
ecosystem. With policy 
measures supporting 
local sourcing, cell 
manufacturing and 
value addition, the 
sector is witnessing the 
development of a more 
integrated and resilient 
supply chain across the 
solar value chain.

Architecting our future through 
strategic financial stewardshipOUR GROWTH STRATEGYScaling Manufacturing Leadership
Expanding solar cell and module capacities to address rising 
domestic demand, strengthen our market presence and enhance 
integrated manufacturing capabilities. Capacity is targeted to 
increase from 10.3 GW modules and 2.94 GW cells to 16.3 GW 
modules and 8.94 GW cells by H1 FY28.
Deepening Backward Integration
Advancing upstream integration through wafer manufacturing and 
localisation of key components to improve supply-chain resilience, 
optimise costs and capture greater value across the production cycle.

across DCR-linked opportunities.

installations to create safer and more sustainable public spaces.

FY2026 At a Glance
FY2026 marked a transformational year for Emmvee 
Photovoltaic Power Limited, characterised by significant 
capacity expansion, strong operational execution, robust 
profitability growth and a strengthened balance sheet 
following its successful public listing. The Company 
expanded its manufacturing footprint, enhanced utilisation 
levels across its integrated operations and nearly doubled its 
order book, positioning itself for the next phase of growth 
in India’s rapidly expanding solar manufacturing ecosystem.Economy Overview
India's economic and industrial growth continues to provide a 
favourable backdrop for the renewable energy sector. While the 
global economy remained resilient, growing by 3.5% in CY20251, 
the operating environment was characterised by heightened 
trade tensions, evolving tariff regimes and geopolitical 
uncertainties that affected global supply chains and commodity 
markets. These developments are particularly relevant for solar 
manufacturers, given the industry's dependence on international 
sourcing of raw materials, components and equipment. Volatility 
in crude oil prices and shipping costs, driven by disruptions in 
key trade routes and geopolitical developments in West Asia, also 
influenced logistics costs and supply chain planning.
India remained one of the fastest-growing major economies 
globally, with GDP growth estimated at 7.7%2 in FY2026. Strong 
economic activity, expanding manufacturing output, infrastructure 
development, urbanisation and rising digitalisation continued 
to support growth in electricity consumption across industrial, 
commercial and residential segments. As power demand 
increases, renewable energy is expected to play an increasingly 
important role in meeting incremental capacity requirements, 
supported by India's target of achieving 500 GW of non-fossil 
fuel capacity by FY2030.3
The country's energy transition agenda, supported by initiatives 
such as ALMM, Production Linked Incentive (PLI) schemes, 
PM Surya Ghar and PM-KUSUM, continues to accelerate solar 
deployment while promoting domestic manufacturing. This creates a favourable operating environment for integrated solar 
manufacturers by supporting both renewable energy capacity 
additions and localisation across the solar value chain.
India's growing power demand, increasing renewable energy 
penetration and policy-led manufacturing support collectively 
provide long-term demand visibility for solar modules, cells and 
related components.
Outlook
The near-term macroeconomic environment is expected to 
remain supportive, although global growth is projected to 
moderate to 3.0% in CY2026, reflecting ongoing trade frictions, 
geopolitical uncertainties and softer demand in certain regions. 
For the solar manufacturing industry, key external variables will 
include global tariff developments, foreign exchange movements, 
commodity prices and logistics costs.
India is expected to remain among the world's fastest-growing 
major economies, with GDP growth projected at 6.6% in FY2027. 
Continued public capital expenditure, manufacturing-led growth, 
expanding power demand and supportive clean energy policies 
are expected to sustain investments across the renewable energy 
value chain. While periodic volatility in raw material prices, freight 
costs and currency movements may persist, the long-term 
outlook remains favourable, supported by increasing localisation, 
rising energy demand and India's accelerating transition towards 
renewable energy.

Industry Overview 
Indian Renewable Energy Sector
India’s renewable energy sector has emerged as a prominent force 
of its energy transition strategy, driven by rising power demand, 
energy security concerns and climate commitments. The country 
has achieved a significant milestone by crossing 50% of installed 
power capacity from non-fossil fuel sources ahead of its 2030 
target, reflecting accelerated adoption of clean energy.
India currently ranks among the top global renewable energy 
markets, supported by strong policy frameworks, competitive 
tariff regimes and increasing private sector participation. The 
renewable energy mix is dominated by solar and wind, with 
solar emerging as the fastest-growing segment due to its 
scalability and declining costs. The sector is also witnessing 
increasing integration of storage solutions, hybrid projects and 
green hydrogen initiatives, further strengthening its long-term 
growth trajectory.
Outlook
India’s renewable energy sector is expected to witness sustained 
and accelerated growth over the medium to long term, supported 
by strong policy backing, improving cost economics and rising 
electricity demand. The country is targeting 500 GW of non-
fossil fuel capacity by 2030, implying significant annual capacity 
additions across solar, wind and hybrid segments.
Solar energy is expected to remain the primary growth driver, 
complemented by wind and emerging technologies such as 
energy storage and green hydrogen. Increasing electrification, corporate sustainability commitments and favourable regulatory 
frameworks are expected to further strengthen demand.
While challenges such as grid integration, land availability and discom 
health persist, ongoing investments in infrastructure and policy reforms 
are likely to address these issues progressively. Overall, the sector is 
well-positioned to play a central role in India’s energy transition, offering 
strong long-term growth visibility and investment opportunities.
Indian Solar Photovoltaic Industry
India's solar photovoltaic (PV) industry has emerged as one of the 
fastest-growing segments within the renewable energy sector, 
supported by rising electricity demand, strong policy support, 
improving cost competitiveness and increasing focus on domestic 
manufacturing. The sector is witnessing rapid expansion across 
utility-scale, rooftop and open-access segments, while simultaneous 
investments in manufacturing capabilities are strengthening India's 
position within the global solar value chain. According to the Central 
Electricity Authority's National Generation Adequacy Plan, electricity 
demand is projected to reach 3,365 BU, while peak demand is 
expected to rise to 459 GW by FY2036. Solar power is expected to 
account for 509 GW of installed capacity by FY2036, reinforcing its 
role as the primary driver of India's energy transition.4
In parallel with rising domestic installations, India is rapidly 
emerging as a global solar manufacturing hub, supported by 
localisation policies, capacity expansion across the value chain 
and increasing diversification of global supply chains.

Domestic Policy Environment
Government policy continues to play a pivotal role in shaping the 
growth trajectory of the solar industry. India has adopted a multi-
pronged strategy aimed at accelerating solar deployment while 
simultaneously promoting domestic manufacturing and reducing 
import dependence.
Key policy initiatives include the Renewable Purchase Obligation 
(RPO) trajectory, which targets a renewable energy share of 
approximately 43% by FY2030, the government's annual 50 
GW renewable energy tendering programme, and the Green 
Energy Open Access Rules, which facilitate renewable power 
procurement by commercial and industrial consumers.
On the manufacturing side, the Approved List of Models and 
Manufacturers (ALMM), the proposed ALMM-II domestic cell 
requirement, and the Production Linked Incentive (PLI) Scheme are 
encouraging localisation and investments across the solar value chain. 
In parallel, schemes such as PM Surya Ghar, PM-KUSUM and various 
government-led procurement programmes are creating sustained 
demand for domestically manufactured modules and cells. Together, 
these initiatives are strengthening domestic manufacturing capabilities 
while enhancing long-term demand visibility for industry participants.
Technology Transition
The Indian solar PV industry is undergoing a rapid technology 
transformation aimed at improving efficiency, reducing lifecycle 
costs and enhancing energy generation.
The sector has largely transitioned away from multi-crystalline 
technologies towards higher-efficiency module architectures. 
The share of Mono PERC technology increased from 
approximately 16% in FY2022 to 63% in FY2025, while TOPCon Source: Government of India Ministry of Power - Central Electricity Authority6
technology has gained significant momentum due to its 
superior efficiency, higher energy yield and improved long-term  
performance characteristics.

Over the medium term, TOPCon is expected to become the 
dominant technology platform across new manufacturing 
investments. Simultaneously, manufacturers are investing 
in automation, advanced quality control systems and next-
generation cell architectures to improve yields and enhance 
competitiveness. These technological advancements are 
expected to support higher module efficiencies, lower balance-
of-system costs and improved project economics across the 
solar ecosystem.
Supply-Chain Economics
India's solar manufacturing ecosystem has expanded significantly 
over the past few years. Module manufacturing capacity 
increased from approximately 21 GW in FY2022 to 82 GW by 
FY2025, while cell manufacturing capacity expanded from 3.2 
GW to 23 GW during the same period.
Despite these additions, the industry continues to exhibit limited 
backward integration into upstream segments such as wafers and polysilicon, resulting in continued reliance on imports for 
critical components. Domestic cell integration remains relatively 
low at approximately 25–30%, contributing to imports of around  
INR 39,00,000 Lakhs between FY2023 and FY2025.
Industry economics remain sensitive to raw material prices, foreign 
exchange movements, freight costs and global trade dynamics, 
with raw materials accounting for approximately 80–85% of 
cell manufacturing costs. Consequently, backward integration 
is increasingly becoming a strategic priority for manufacturers 
seeking to improve supply security, enhance margins and reduce 
exposure to external volatility.

Looking ahead, India's manufacturing base is expected to 
strengthen considerably, with module manufacturing capacity 
projected to reach 175–185 GW by FY2030, accompanied 
by increasing investments in cells and upstream components. 
Supported by policy measures, localisation initiatives and 
strong domestic demand, India is expected to strengthen its 
position as a globally competitive solar manufacturing hub over 
the medium term.
Industry Implications for Emmvee
	 Strong domestic demand supports long-term 
capacity utilisation.

	 Export diversification creates additional 
growth opportunities.Company Overview
Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited (Emmvee) is a leading 
integrated solar PV module and solar cell      manufacturing 
company in India and among the largest pure-play solar module 
and cell manufacturers, with a strong focus on advanced 
photovoltaic technologies. The Company has established a 
robust manufacturing ecosystem, with multiple facilities located 
in close proximity in Bengaluru, enabling operational efficiency 
and streamlined logistics.
Emmvee operates an integrated manufacturing model spanning 
solar PV modules and solar cells, supported by strong backward 
integration initiatives and strategic sourcing capabilities. Its 
manufacturing capacity has scaled significantly throughout the 
year, positioning the Company as a key participant in India’s 
renewable energy transition.
The Company benefits from early adoption of high-efficiency 
TOPCon technology, supported by its collaboration with 
Fraunhofer ISE, enhancing product performance and 
competitiveness. Its integrated operations, along with increasing 
utilisation of captive cells, support margin expansion and 
cost optimisation.
Emmvee serves a diversified customer base, including 
independent power producers (IPPs), commercial & industrial 
(C&I) customers and government entities with a strong order 
book providing medium-term revenue visibility. The Company 
continues to expand its domestic and international presence 
through capacity additions, increased backward integration and 
a focus on next-generation solar technologies.

Highlights During the Year
Capacity Expansion and Manufacturing Scale-Up
	 Commissioned two new 2.5 GW solar PV module 
manufacturing lines at Sulibele, increasing total module 
manufacturing capacity from 5.3 GW to 10.3 GW*. 
	 Maintained solar cell manufacturing capacity at 2.94 GW, 
creating a stronger foundation for integrated operations. 
	 Continued expansion of TOPCon-based manufacturing 
capabilities to support next-generation solar technologies. 
Production Growth and Improved Capacity Utilisation
	 Module production increased to 2,999 MW, supported by 
capacity ramp-up and strong customer demand. 
	 Cell production increased to 1,520 MW, reflecting improved 
operational efficiency and higher captive consumption. 
	 Cell capacity utilisation improved to approximately 
70%, demonstrating successful ramp-up of integrated 
manufacturing operations.
Strong Commercial Momentum
	 Order book increased from approximately 4.9 GW to 9.4 
GW, providing strong medium-term revenue visibility. 
	 Secured a 4.5 GW long-term TOPCon cell supply 
agreement, strengthening future demand visibility. 
	 Recorded order inflows of approximately 7.74 
GW during FY2026.
Robust Financial Performance
	 Revenue from operations increased to INR 5,04,988 Lakhs. 
	 EBITDA grew to INR 1,73,437 Lakhs, with EBITDA margin 
remaining strong at 34%. 	 Profit after tax increased to INR 1,08,155 Lakhs, supported by 
operating leverage, higher utilisation and integrated operations.
	 Return on Capital Employed (ROCE) improved to 38%.
Balance Sheet Strengthening and Leverage Reduction
	 Successfully completed the Initial Public Offering (IPO), 
raising approximately INR 2,90,000 Lakhs. 
	 Utilised IPO proceeds to prepay INR 1,62,129 Lakhs of  
borrowings. 
	 Transitioned to a net cash position, with Net Debt-to-Equity 
improving to (0.06)x. 
	 ICRA upgraded the Company’s long-term and short-
term credit ratings to A/A2+, reflecting its improved 
financial strength. 
Working Capital and Operational Efficiency
	 Improved inventory planning and production scheduling 
supported efficient execution of the expanding order book. 
	 Continued optimisation of material consumption through 
process improvements and reduction in silver paste usage. 
	 Focused on enhancing operational productivity and 
manufacturing yields across facilities.
Strategic Capex and Backward Integration Pipeline
	 Commenced development of the 6 GW integrated cell and 
module manufacturing facility at Devanahalli. 
	 Completed land acquisition and secured funding support, 
including a sanction from IREDA. 
	 Announced plans to expand module and cell capacities to 
16.3 GW and 8.94 GW, respectively, by FY2028. 
	 Initiated evaluation of a 9 GW ingot and wafer manufacturing 
programme to strengthen backward integration and 
supply-chain security.*Capacity adjusted for discontinuation of Unit IBusiness Vertical Performance
Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited operates as an integrated solar manufacturer, with revenue primarily derived from the sale of solar 
PV modules and solar cells. The Company’s performance is driven by capacity expansion, improving utilisation levels and an optimised 
product mix, particularly with increased use of captive cell production.
During the period, the Company reported strong revenue growth, driven by higher dispatch volumes and the ramp-up of newly 
commissioned capacities. The module segment remains the primary revenue contributor, while the cell segment plays a strategic role 
in enhancing integration and supporting margin improvement.

Manufacturing
Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited operates an integrated solar 
manufacturing platform encompassing both solar PV modules 
and solar cells. Its manufacturing facilities are strategically 
clustered in Karnataka, enabling operational synergies, efficient 
logistics and enhanced control over production processes. The 
Company continues to expand its manufacturing footprint while 
focusing on technology upgradation, process efficiency and 
backward integration to strengthen competitiveness.

Notes
1. Return on Capital Employed (ROCE) = Earnings Before Interest and Taxes (EBIT) divided by Capital Employed. Capital Employed 
is calculated as the sum of total equity, total debt and deferred tax liability, net of deferred tax assets. 
2. Return on Equity (ROE) = Profit after tax attributable to owners of the Company divided by Average Equity. Average Equity is 
calculated as the average of opening and closing total equity attributable to owners. 
3. Current Ratio = Current Assets divided by Current Liabilities. 
4. Debt-Equity Ratio = Total Borrowings (current and non-current) divided by Total Equity attributable to owners of the Company. 
5. Net Profit Margin = Profit after Tax divided by Revenue from Operations. 
6. Trade Receivables Turnover Ratio = Revenue From Operations divided by Average Trade Receivables
7. Inventory Turnover Ratio = Cost of Goods Sold (COGS) divided by Average Inventory
8. Interest Coverage Ratio = Earnings Before Interest and Tax (EBIT) divided by Finance Costs (Interest Expense)

Human Resource
Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited recognises its employees 
as a key enabler of operational excellence, innovation and 
sustainable growth. As of 31 March 2026, the Company employed 
over 3,800 personnel, including 587 engineers, supporting 
operations across manufacturing, research & development, 
supply chain and commercial functions. The workforce continues 
to expand in line with the Company's capacity additions and 
integrated manufacturing strategy.
The Company focuses on attracting and retaining skilled 
technical talent to support advanced manufacturing 
technologies, including TOPCon-based cell and module 
production. Continuous training and capability-building 
initiatives are undertaken to enhance technical competencies, 
productivity and operational effectiveness across functions. 
Emmvee also promotes a performance-oriented work culture, 
supported by employee engagement initiatives and career 
development opportunities.
Health, safety and workplace well-being remain integral to the 
Company's operating philosophy. The Company continues to 
strengthen safety practices across its manufacturing facilities 
while fostering a collaborative and inclusive work environment. 
Through continued investments in talent development and 
workforce capability enhancement, Emmvee remains committed 
to building a future-ready organisation aligned with its long-term 
growth ambitions.

Outlook
Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited is well positioned to 
capitalise on the structural growth opportunities arising from 
India's accelerating renewable energy transition, supported by 
rising solar installations, increasing localisation and favourable 
policy support for domestic manufacturing. The Company 
remains focused on strengthening its position as an integrated 
solar manufacturing platform through capacity expansion, 
technology advancement and value-chain integration.
As part of its next phase of growth, Emmvee is progressing 
with the development of its 6 GW integrated cell and module 
manufacturing facility at Devanahalli, which is expected to increase 
total installed capacities to 16.3 GW of modules and 8.94 GW of 
cells. The project is being executed in line with planned timelines, 
with land acquisition completed and IREDA having sanctioned a 
term loan of INR 3,30,600 Lakhs to support the expansion.The Company has outlined a capital expenditure programme of 
approximately INR 5,50,000 Lakhs, including hard costs of around 
INR 4,60,000 Lakhs towards the integrated manufacturing facility, 
reflecting its commitment to scaling operations while strengthening 
its integrated manufacturing capabilities. In parallel, Emmvee is 
evaluating phased expansion into upstream manufacturing through 
the development of up to 9 GW of ingot and wafer capacity, which 
is expected to enhance supply-chain resilience, reduce import 
dependence and improve value capture across the solar value chain. 
Management has indicated that ingot and wafer manufacturing 
would require capital expenditure of approximately INR 60,000–
70,000 Lakhs per GW of capacity. 
Operationally, the Company will continue to focus on improving 
utilisation across existing capacities, increasing captive cell 
consumption, enhancing process efficiencies and expanding 
adoption of advanced technologies such as TOPCon and G12R. 
A strong order book of approximately 9.4 GW, including multi-
year cell supply contracts, provides revenue visibility and supports 
future capacity ramp-up.
Overall, Emmvee's strategy remains centred on building a fully 
integrated, technology-driven and globally competitive solar 
manufacturing platform, supported by disciplined capital 
allocation, balance-sheet strength and sustained investments in 
scale, efficiency and backward integration.
Internal Control and Its Adequacy  
The Company has established robust internal control systems 
commensurate with the scale and complexity of its operations, 
covering key areas such as procurement, manufacturing, 
inventory, finance and compliance, supported by defined policies 
and standard operating procedures. An independent internal 
audit function periodically reviews the effectiveness of these 
controls, with findings placed before the Audit Committee for 
oversight and corrective action. The Company also leverages 
IT systems to enhance process efficiency, data integrity and 
monitoring of operations. Based on periodic reviews, the internal 
control framework is considered adequate and effective, with no 
material weaknesses observed during the year.

PERFORMANCE HIGHLIGHTS AND STATE OF THE 
COMPANY’S AFFAIRS:
Consolidated Performance
During the financial year 2025-2026, the Company has delivered 
strong growth in revenue from operations of H5,04,987.73 lakhs 
as compared to H2,33,561.34 lakhs in the previous financial year, 
registered a growth of 116.21% on a year-on-year basis. The Earnings 
Before Interest, Tax, Depreciation and Amortization (EBITDA) for 
current year is H 1,73,437.47 lakhs as compared to H 72,193.80 lakhs 
in the previous financial year, registered a growth of 140.24% on a 
year-on-year basis. Further, Profit Before Tax (PBT) is H1,33,759.53 
lakhs as compared to H48,282.03 lakhs in the previous financial 
year and Profit After Tax (PAT) is H1,08,155.15 lakhs as compared to 
H36,901.44 lakhs in the previous financial year, registered a growth 
of 177.04% and 193.09% respectively on a year-on-year basis.
During the year under report, the Company through its wholly 
owned subsidiary- Emmvee Energy Private Limited, successfully 
commissioned module manufacturing facilities with an installed 
capacity of 5.01 GW at its factory premises located at Unit-5 
and Unit-6, Kadaranaga Village, Sulibele Hobli, Hoskote Taluk, 
Bengaluru Rural, Hosakote, Bengaluru, Rural - 562129. With 
these additions, the Company’s total module manufacturing 
capacity (including the Indian subsidiary) as on March 31, 2026 
stood at 10.3 GW. Further, the Company through its wholly 
owned subsidiary- Emmvee Energy Private Limited, has a cell 
manufacturing capacity of 2.94 GW as on March 31, 2026. 
The management is confident that commissioning of these new capacities has enhanced the manufacturing capability and will 
enable the Company in driving long term growth.
Standalone Performance
During the financial year 2025-26, the Company reported 
revenue from operations of H 1,07,455.87 lakhs as compared to 
H 1,76,348.12 lakhs in the previous financial year representing 
a decrease of 39.07% on a year-on-year basis. The Earnings 
Before Interest, Tax, Depreciation and Amortization (EBITDA) for 
the current year were H13,013.60 lakhs. Further, the Company 
recorded a Profit Before Tax (PBT) of H21,190.50 lakhs and a Profit 
After Tax (PAT) of H 15,574.87. 
HIGHLIGHTS OF PERFORMANCE OF SUBSIDIARIES 
AND THEIR CONTRIBUTION TO THE OVERALL 
PERFORMANCE OF THE COMPANY:
Emmvee Energy Private Limited (EEPL), the Company’s wholly 
owned subsidiary, delivered robust growth during the Financial 
Year 2025-26, driven by commissioning of new manufacturing 
capacities and scale-up of operations. EEPL recorded a substantial 
increase in EBITDA, PBT and PAT during the year, reflecting 
improved operational efficiency and higher production volumes. 
While EBITDA margins moderated marginally due to capacity  
ramp-up, overall profitability improved on account of better cost 
absorption and operational leverage. The overseas subsidiaries, 
Emmvee Energy GmbH (Germany) and Emmvee Energy Inc. 
(USA), continued to support the Company’s global presence and 
business development initiatives.

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS 
REPORT:

PREVENTION OF SEXUAL HARASSMENT AT 
WORKPLACE:
As per the requirements under the Sexual Harassment of Women 
at Workplace (Prevention, Prohibition & Redressal) Act, 2013 
(‘POSH Act’) and Rules made thereunder, the Company has 
constituted Internal Complaints Committee (‘ICC’). To build 
awareness in this area, the Company has been conducting 
necessary training in the organization on an ongoing basis. 
The Company has appointed the following members 
of ICC as below: 
1. A Presiding Officer or Chairperson who is a woman 
employed at a senior level at the workplace from 
amongst the employees.
2. The Company has appointed an external independent person 
committed to this cause and who has the requisite experience 
in handling such matters, as other members of ICC. 
3. Not less than two members from amongst employees are 
committed to the cause of women; their safety and have 
experience in social work and have legal knowledge.

PROCEEDINGS PENDING UNDER THE INSOLVENCY 
AND BANKRUPTCY CODE, 2016:
During the year under review, the Company has initiated insolvency 
proceedings under the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 
("IBC") against Bundelkhand Saur Urja Limited ("BSUL") by filing 
an application under Section 9 of the IBC before the Hon'ble 
National Company Law Tribunal, Allahabad Bench, on 27 April 
2026. The application seeks initiation of the Corporate Insolvency 
Resolution Process ("CIRP") in respect of an operational debt of 
INR 2019.62 Lakhs (Rupees Twenty Crores Nineteen Lakhs Sixty-
One Thousand Five Hundred and Forty-Seven only).
BSUL, a joint venture between NHPC Limited and UPNEDA, 
had undertaken the development of a 65 MW Solar PV Power 
Plant at the Kalpi Solar Power Project. The Company supplied 
photovoltaic modules for the project, however, the outstanding 
consideration remains unpaid despite repeated demands and 
commitments by BSUL.
The matter is currently pending adjudication before the Hon'ble 
National Company Law Tribunal, Allahabad Bench and is in 
the final stages of determination. Other than the aforesaid 
proceeding, no insolvency proceedings under the IBC have been 
initiated by or against the Company during the year under review.
DISCLOSURES PERTAINING TO COMPLIANCE 
WITH THE PROVISIONS OF THE MATERNITY 
BENEFIT ACT, 1961:
The Company has complied with the provisions of the Maternity 
Benefit Act, 1961, including all applicable amendments and rules framed thereunder. The Company is committed to ensuring a 
safe, inclusive, and supportive workplace for women employees. 
All eligible women employees are provided with maternity benefits 
as prescribed under the Maternity Benefit Act, 1961.

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT
1. COMPANY’S PHILOSOPHY ON CORPORATE GOVERNANCE:
 Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited (“Emmvee”/“the Company”) believes in following the highest standards of corporate 
governance based on transparency, integrity, accountability and fairness. Our governance framework seeks to safeguard 
stakeholder interests while enabling sustainable growth in the Indian renewables manufacturing ecosystem. The Board provides 
strategic oversight supported by well-defined charters for Board committees, robust risk management, and rigorous compliance 
with the SEBI (LODR) Regulations, 2015.
2. BOARD OF DIRECTORS:
2.1 ROLE OF BOARD OF DIRECTORS:
 The Board of Directors of the Company have a fiduciary responsibility to ensure that the Company’s actions and objectives are 
aligned to sustainable growth and long-term value creation. The Board also provides strategic direction, reviews and approves 
management’s business objectives, plans and oversees risk management. It also sets out standards of corporate behaviour and 
ensures compliance with laws and regulations impacting the Company’s business.
2.2 COMPOSITION AND CATEGORY OF DIRECTORS AS ON MARCH 31, 2026:
 As on March 31, 2026, the Board of Directors comprised of six Directors, each bringing significant experience in their respective fields. The 
Board includes two Executive Directors, one Non-Executive Non-independent Director and three Non-Executive Independent Directors, 
including one Woman Independent Director. This composition reflects a well-balanced mix of professionalism, expertise, and diverse 
knowledge. The Chairman of the Board is an Executive Director. The composition of the Board conforms to Regulation 17 of the SEBI 
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015.
 Pursuant to section 152(6) of the Companies Act, 2013, two directors on the Board i.e., Mrs. Shubha Manjunatha Donthi, Non-Executive 
Non-Independent Director and Mr. Suhas Donthi Manjunatha Executive Non-Independent Director are liable to retire by rotation as per 
their terms of appointment.

provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, read with the Companies

Securities and Exchange Board of India (Share Based Employee

Independent Auditor’s Report
Key Audit Matters How the Key Audit Matters was addressed in our audit
Capital expenditure in respect of Property Plant and 
Equipment (PPE) and Capital Work-in-Progress (CWIP)
The Group has significant additions (in one of the subsidiary) 
during the year in the PPE and CWIP with respect to solar 
module and cells plants. These projects require a substantial 
period of time to be ready for their intended use.
The expenditure incurred on these projects requires careful 
evaluation of the nature of costs to ensure that their capitalisation 
under Property, Plant and Equipment is in accordance with the 
Group’s accounting policy and applicable accounting standards. 
Further, the determination of the useful economic lives assigned 
by management involves estimation and judgment.
Considering the significance of the amounts involved, the 
judgment required in determining the eligibility of costs for 
capitalisation, the estimation involved in assessing useful lives, 
and the risk of management override, we have identified capital 
expenditure relating to Property, Plant and Equipment and 
Capital Work-in-Progress as a key audit matter.Our audit procedures include the following:
1. Obtained understanding of the Group’s capitalisation policy and 
assessed for compliance with the relevant accounting standards.
2. Evaluated the design and tested the operating effectiveness of 
controls related to capital expenditure and capitalisation of assets.
3. Performed substantive testing on a sample basis for nature 
of direct and indirect cost attributable to these projects 
including verification of underlying supporting evidence and 
understanding nature of the costs capitalised.
4. Obtained understanding on management assessment relating 
to progress of projects and their intention to bring the asset to 
its intended use.
5. Obtained understanding of the assessment made by the 
management for estimation of useful life.
6. Obtained board minutes to sight the overall approvals provided 
by the Board of Directors for capital expenditure.To 
The Members of

Report on the Audit of the Consolidated Financial 
Statements
Opinion
We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial 
statements of Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Limited (Formerly 
known as Emmvee Photovoltaic Power Private Limited)  (hereinafter 
referred to as the “Holding Company”) and its subsidiaries 
(Holding Company and its subsidiaries together referred to as 
“the Group”), which comprise the Consolidated Balance Sheet as 
at March 31, 2026, and the Consolidated Statement of Profit and 
Loss (including Other Comprehensive Income), the Consolidated 
Statement of Changes in Equity and the Consolidated Statement 
of Cash Flows for the year then ended, and notes to the 
consolidated financial statements, including material accounting 
policy information and other explanatory information (hereinafter 
referred to as “the consolidated financial statements”).
In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to 
the explanations given to us, the aforesaid consolidated financial 
statements give the information required by the Companies Act, 
2013 (“the Act”) in the manner so required and give a true and 
fair view in conformity with the Indian Accounting Standards 
prescribed under section 133 of the Act read with Companies 
(Indian Accounting Standards) Rules, 2015, as amended (“Ind 
AS”) and other accounting principles generally accepted in India, 
of their consolidated state of affairs of the Group as at March 
31, 2026, of consolidated profit (including other comprehensive income),consolidated changes in equity and its consolidated 
cash flows for the year ended on that date.
Basis for Opinion
We conducted our audit of the consolidated financial statements 
in accordance with the Standards on Auditing (SAs), as specified 
under section 143(10) of the Act. Our responsibilities under those 
SAs are further described in the Auditor’s Responsibilities for the 
Audit of the Consolidated Financial Statements section of our 
report. We are independent of the Group, in accordance with 
the ethical requirements that are relevant to our audit of the 
consolidated financial statements in terms of the Code of Ethics 
issued by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India, and 
the relevant provisions of the Act and we have fulfilled our other 
ethical responsibilities in accordance with these requirements. 
We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained, is sufficient 
and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion.
Key Audit Matters
Key audit matters are those matters that, in our professional 
judgment, were of most significance in our audit of the 
consolidated financial statements of the current period. These 
matters were addressed in the context of our audit of the 
consolidated financial statements as a whole, and in forming our 
opinion thereon, and we do not provide a separate opinion on 
these matters. We have determined the matters described below 
to be the key audit matters to be communicated in our report:Key Audit Matters How the Key Audit Matters was addressed in our audit
Refer Note 2.3(a) and 3 to the consolidated financial statements. 7. Assessed the required disclosures made by in the consolidated 
financial statements for compliance with the requirement of Ind 
AS and Schedule III of the Companies Act 2013.
Revenue recognition from the sale of goods
The Group’s revenue is derived primarily from sale of goods. 
Revenue from sale of goods is recognised when control of the 
products sold is transferred to the customer and there are no 
longer any unfulfilled performance obligations.
The performance obligations in the contracts are fulfilled at the 
time of dispatch, delivery or upon formal customer acceptance 
depending on customer terms.
Inappropriate assessment could lead to risk of revenue being 
recognized before transfer of control.
In view of the above and since revenue is a key performance 
indicator of the Group, we have identified timing of revenue 
recognition at period end from sale of goods as a key audit 
matter.
Refer Note 2.3(k) and 22 to the consolidated financial statements.Our audit procedures include the following:
1. Assessed the appropriateness and consistency of Group’s 
revenue recognition policy as per IND AS 115 Revenue from 
contract with customers.
2. Obtained an understanding and assessed the design, 
implementation, and operating effectiveness of controls over 
recognition and measurement of revenue in accordance with 
customer contracts, including timing of revenue recognition.
3. Performed substantive testing by selecting samples of revenue 
transactions recorded during the year and verifying the 
underlying documents which include purchase orders from 
customers, invoices and proof of deliveries.
4. Performed the cut-off testing of the revenue recorded in the 
appropriate period and traced the sales with invoice copy, proof 
of delivery, customer acknowledgement, and lorry receipts to 
confirm the appropriateness of recognition of revenue for that 
period in the books of accounts.
5. Obtained management representation that revenue has been 
recorded as per the requirements of Ind AS 115.
6. Assessed the required disclosures made by in the consolidated 
financial statements for compliance with the requirement of Ind 
AS and Schedule III of the Companies Act 2013.
Information Other than the Consolidated Financial 
Statements and Auditor’s Report Thereon

the other information. The other information comprises the 
information included in the Annual Report, Director’s report, 
Chairman’s statement, Business Responsibility and Sustainability 
Reporting but does not include the consolidated financial 
statements and our auditor’s report thereon.
Our opinion on the consolidated financial statements does not 
cover the other information and we do not and will not express

In connection with our audit of the consolidated financial

identified above and, in doing so, consider whether the other

otherwise appears to be materially misstated.
When we read the Annual Report, Director’s report, Chairman’s

Consolidated Financial Statements
Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether 
the consolidated financial statements as a whole are free from 
material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to 
issue an auditor’s report that includes our opinion. Reasonable 
assurance is a high level of assurance, but is not a guarantee that 
an audit conducted in accordance with SAs will always detect 
a material misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise 
from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or 
in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence

Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of the

As part of an audit in accordance with SAs, we exercise 
professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism 
throughout the audit. We also:
	 Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the 
consolidated financial statements, whether due to fraud or 
error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to 
those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and 
appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of 
not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is 
higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve 
collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, 
or the override of internal control.
	 Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the 
audit in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate 
in the circumstances. Under section 143(3)(i) of the Act, we 
are also responsible for expressing our opinion on whether 
the Holding Company has adequate internal financial 
controls with reference to consolidated financial statements 
in place and the operating effectiveness of such controls.
	 Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies 
used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates 
and related disclosures made by the Management and 
Board of Directors.
	 Conclude on the appropriateness of the Management 
and Board of Directors use of the going concern basis of 
accounting and, based on the audit evidence obtained, 
whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or 
conditions that may cast significant doubt on the ability 
of the Group and to continue as a going concern. If we 
conclude that a material uncertainty exists, we are required 
to draw attention in our auditor’s report to the related 
disclosures in the consolidated financial statements or, if 
such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our 
conclusions are based on the audit evidence obtained up 
to the date of our auditor’s report. However, future events 
or conditions may cause the Group to cease to continue as 
a going concern.
	 Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and content 
of the consolidated financial statements, including the 
disclosures, and whether the consolidated financial 
statements represent the underlying transactions and events 
in a manner that achieves fair presentation.	 Obtain sufficient appropriate audit evidence regarding the 
financial information of the entities or business activities 
within the Group to express an opinion on the consolidated 
financial statements. We are responsible for the direction, 
supervision and performance of the audit of the financial 
statements of such entities included in the consolidated 
financial statements of which we are the independent 
auditors. For the other entities or business activities included 
in the consolidated financial statements, which have been 
audited by other auditors, such other auditors remain 
responsible for the direction, supervision and performance 
of the audits carried out by them. We remain solely 
responsible for our audit opinion.
We communicate with those charged with governance of

 We also provide those charged with governance with a statement
